Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t like saying it that way, because it sounds like I just stopped caring, and I didn’t mean to.

And work has been affected too. I’ve gotten written warnings because I’ve missed shifts and turned in inspection paperwork late. It’s like things I used to keep up with or care about are just… slipping, I guess.

Maybe a little sometimes, but I can’t really tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and still find pleasure in now?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can name one clearly. Like, I used to do little things, you know, watch shows, go out with people sometimes, church stuff, maybe just sitting around talking—but now when I do them, I’m kind of half there.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about random things, like whether I sorted something wrong at work or if I left my clothes in the washer too long, and it’s like the moment passes before I can tell if I was enjoying it. So… I don’t know. I don’t really have a good answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t like saying it that way, because it sounds like I just stopped caring, and I didn’t mean to.\n\nBut I’ve mostly stopped going to basketball and the dance nights, and I used to actually look forward to those. And the church STEM mentoring thing too—I cared about that a lot, like helping the kids with little projects and stuff. I even bought these markers once because the regular ones were all dried out, and then I never brought them because I didn’t go that week… or the next.\n\nAnd work has been affected too. I’ve gotten written warnings because I’ve missed shifts and turned in inspection paperwork late. It’s like things I used to keep up with or care about are just… slipping, I guess.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly.

I mean, sometimes when everything feels piled up, I’ll think things like I wish I could just not have to deal with any of it for a while, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you mean. Like, not wake up, or harming myself… I’m not sure. I don’t really sit there and make a plan or anything—sorry, I’m kind of getting stuck on the wording.

It’s hard because I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I left something unfinished at work, and then I don’t even know what I was feeling in the first place. So… I guess I’m not sure.

I’m sorry, I know you’re asking directly. I don’t… I don’t think I can give a clean yes or no.

Maybe a little sometimes, but I can’t really tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and still find pleasure in now?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can name one clearly. Like, I used to do little things, you know, watch shows, go out with people sometimes, church stuff, maybe just sitting around talking—but now when I do them, I’m kind of half there.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about random things, like whether I sorted something wrong at work or if I left my clothes in the washer too long, and it’s like the moment passes before I can tell if I was enjoying it. So… I don’t know. I don’t really have a good answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t like saying it that way, because it sounds like I just stopped caring, and I didn’t mean to.\n\nBut I’ve mostly stopped going to basketball and the dance nights, and I used to actually look forward to those. And the church STEM mentoring thing too—I cared about that a lot, like helping the kids with little projects and stuff. I even bought these markers once because the regular ones were all dried out, and then I never brought them because I didn’t go that week… or the next.\n\nAnd work has been affected too. I’ve gotten written warnings because I’ve missed shifts and turned in inspection paperwork late. It’s like things I used to keep up with or care about are just… slipping, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly.\n\nI mean, sometimes when everything feels piled up, I’ll think things like I wish I could just not have to deal with any of it for a while, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you mean. Like, not wake up, or harming myself… I’m not sure. I don’t really sit there and make a plan or anything—sorry, I’m kind of getting stuck on the wording.\n\nIt’s hard because I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I left something unfinished at work, and then I don’t even know what I was feeling in the first place. So… I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re asking directly. I don’t… I don’t think I can give a clean yes or no.\n\nSometimes I get really overwhelmed and I have thoughts that scare me a little, but then I start wondering if I mean “harm myself” or if I just mean I want everything to stop for a bit. And then my brain goes off to something stupid, like whether I forgot to submit a form at work, and I lose track of what I was even trying to say.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really sorted it out in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “end my life” sounds so definite, and I don’t know if what I have counts as that.\n\nSometimes I get this feeling like I just want everything to stop, like I want to disappear from all the noise and expectations for a while. I mean, I still do some things because they’re part of my routine, like going where I’m supposed to go, seeing people when I need to… but whether I enjoy them like before, I’m not sure.\n\nIt’s kind of hard to compare, because “before” feels blurry. Like, I remember I used to be more… I don’t know, lighter maybe. But I can’t really pin down if it’s the things themselves or just me being tired of everything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you currently find pleasure in any activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… sometimes I’ll do things because they’re there, like put on a show or go along if someone invites me somewhere, but I don’t know if I’d call it pleasure exactly.\n\nIt’s weird, because people will say, “Oh, wasn’t that fun?” and I’ll kind of agree, but then I’m thinking about how I need to do laundry or whether I forgot something at work. At my job, we have to check tiny details over and over, and I guess my brain gets stuck like that even outside of work. So… I don’t know. Maybe a little sometimes, but I can’t really tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and still find pleasure in now?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can name one clearly. Like, I used to do little things, you know, watch shows, go out with people sometimes, church stuff, maybe just sitting around talking—but now when I do them, I’m kind of half there.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about random things, like whether I sorted something wrong at work or if I left my clothes in the washer too long, and it’s like the moment passes before I can tell if I was enjoying it. So… I don’t know. I don’t really have a good answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t like saying it that way, because it sounds like I just stopped caring, and I didn’t mean to.\n\nBut I’ve mostly stopped going to basketball and the dance nights, and I used to actually look forward to those. And the church STEM mentoring thing too—I cared about that a lot, like helping the kids with little projects and stuff. I even bought these markers once because the regular ones were all dried out, and then I never brought them because I didn’t go that week… or the next.\n\nAnd work has been affected too. I’ve gotten written warnings because I’ve missed shifts and turned in inspection paperwork late. It’s like things I used to keep up with or care about are just… slipping, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly.\n\nI mean, sometimes when everything feels piled up, I’ll think things like I wish I could just not have to deal with any of it for a while, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you mean. Like, not wake up, or harming myself… I’m not sure. I don’t really sit there and make a plan or anything—sorry, I’m kind of getting stuck on the wording.\n\nIt’s hard because I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I left something unfinished at work, and then I don’t even know what I was feeling in the first place. So… I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re asking directly. I don’t… I don’t think I can give a clean yes or no.\n\nSometimes I get really overwhelmed and I have thoughts that scare me a little, but then I start wondering if I mean “harm myself” or if I just mean I want everything to stop for a bit. And then my brain goes off to something stupid, like whether I forgot to submit a form at work, and I lose track of what I was even trying to say.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really sorted it out in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “end my life” sounds so definite, and I don’t know if what I have counts as that.\n\nSometimes I get this feeling like I just want everything to stop, like I want to disappear from all the noise and expectations for a while. But then I start thinking about, like, whether I’m even answering you right, or if I’m making it sound worse than it is, and then my brain jumps to some random thing I need to do later.\n\nSo… I’m not trying to avoid the question. I just don’t feel sure how to answer it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself consistently, but my work pants have been fitting different from month to month, which is kind of how I notice.\n\nIt’s mostly eating being all over the place. Like most days on shift I’ll just not eat until it’s almost over because nothing sounds good, and then other times I end up getting vending machine stuff late because I’m just drained and it’s there. The break room vending machine has these chips I keep buying even though I don’t even really want them, and then I feel weird about it after.\n\nSo, yeah, my weight’s been kind of up and down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed it in a clear way.\n\nLike, at work I’m moving around because I have to, checking things and standing there and doing the same steps over and over, so it’s hard to tell if I’m slower or just… tired of doing it. And sitting still, I mean, I’ll tap my foot sometimes, but I’ve kind of always done that, especially if I’m waiting somewhere.\n\nSorry, I’m probably not answering right. I guess I haven’t paid enough attention to know.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. So… I don’t know. I don’t really have a good answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t like saying it that way, because it sounds like I just stopped caring, and I didn’t mean to.\n\nBut I’ve mostly stopped going to basketball and the dance nights, and I used to actually look forward to those. And the church STEM mentoring thing too—I cared about that a lot, like helping the kids with little projects and stuff. I even bought these markers once because the regular ones were all dried out, and then I never brought them because I didn’t go that week… or the next.\n\nAnd work has been affected too. I’ve gotten written warnings because I’ve missed shifts and turned in inspection paperwork late. It’s like things I used to keep up with or care about are just… slipping, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly.\n\nI mean, sometimes when everything feels piled up, I’ll think things like I wish I could just not have to deal with any of it for a while, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you mean. Like, not wake up, or harming myself… I’m not sure. I don’t really sit there and make a plan or anything—sorry, I’m kind of getting stuck on the wording.\n\nIt’s hard because I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I left something unfinished at work, and then I don’t even know what I was feeling in the first place. So… I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re asking directly. I don’t… I don’t think I can give a clean yes or no.\n\nSometimes I get really overwhelmed and I have thoughts that scare me a little, but then I start wondering if I mean “harm myself” or if I just mean I want everything to stop for a bit. And then my brain goes off to something stupid, like whether I forgot to submit a form at work, and I lose track of what I was even trying to say.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really sorted it out in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “end my life” sounds so definite, and I don’t know if what I have counts as that.\n\nSometimes I get this feeling like I just want everything to stop, like I want to disappear from all the noise and expectations for a while. But then I start thinking about, like, whether I’m even answering you right, or if I’m making it sound worse than it is, and then my brain jumps to some random thing I need to do later.\n\nSo… I’m not trying to avoid the question. I just don’t feel sure how to answer it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Like most days on shift I’ll just not eat until it’s almost over because nothing sounds good, and then other times I end up getting vending machine stuff late because I’m just drained and it’s there. The break room vending machine has these chips I keep buying even though I don’t even really want them, and then I feel weird about it after.\n\nSo, yeah, my weight’s been kind of up and down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed it in a clear way.\n\nLike, at work I’m moving around because I have to, checking things and standing there and doing the same steps over and over, so it’s hard to tell if I’m slower or just… tired of doing it. I guess I haven’t paid enough attention to know.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I mean, I don’t know. I say sorry a lot, if that counts, but sometimes that’s just because it feels like the easiest thing to say.\n\nLike at work, if something gets held up, I’ll kind of assume maybe I missed something, but then there are so many little steps that it’s hard to know whose part was whose. So… I don’t know. I don’t really have a good answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t like saying it that way, because it sounds like I just stopped caring, and I didn’t mean to.\n\nBut I’ve mostly stopped going to basketball and the dance nights, and I used to actually look forward to those. And the church STEM mentoring thing too—I cared about that a lot, like helping the kids with little projects and stuff. I even bought these markers once because the regular ones were all dried out, and then I never brought them because I didn’t go that week… or the next.\n\nAnd work has been affected too. I’ve gotten written warnings because I’ve missed shifts and turned in inspection paperwork late. It’s like things I used to keep up with or care about are just… slipping, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly.\n\nI mean, sometimes when everything feels piled up, I’ll think things like I wish I could just not have to deal with any of it for a while, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you mean. Like, not wake up, or harming myself… I’m not sure. I don’t really sit there and make a plan or anything—sorry, I’m kind of getting stuck on the wording.\n\nIt’s hard because I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I left something unfinished at work, and then I don’t even know what I was feeling in the first place. So… I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re asking directly. I don’t… I don’t think I can give a clean yes or no.\n\nSometimes I get really overwhelmed and I have thoughts that scare me a little, but then I start wondering if I mean “harm myself” or if I just mean I want everything to stop for a bit. And then my brain goes off to something stupid, like whether I forgot to submit a form at work, and I lose track of what I was even trying to say.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really sorted it out in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “end my life” sounds so definite, and I don’t know if what I have counts as that.\n\nSometimes I get this feeling like I just want everything to stop, like I want to disappear from all the noise and expectations for a while. But then I start thinking about, like, whether I’m even answering you right, or if I’m making it sound worse than it is, and then my brain jumps to some random thing I need to do later.\n\nSo… I’m not trying to avoid the question. I just don’t feel sure how to answer it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself consistently, but my work pants have been fitting different from month to month, which is kind of how I notice.\n\nIt’s mostly eating being all over the place. Like most days on shift I’ll just not eat until it’s almost over because nothing sounds good, and then other times I end up getting vending machine stuff late because I’m just drained and it’s there. The break room vending machine has these chips I keep buying even though I don’t even really want them, and then I feel weird about it after.\n\nSo, yeah, my weight’s been kind of up and down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed it in a clear way.\n\nLike, at work I’m moving around because I have to, checking things and standing there and doing the same steps over and over, so it’s hard to tell if I’m slower or just… tired of doing it. And sitting still, I mean, I’ll tap my foot sometimes, but I’ve kind of always done that, especially if I’m waiting somewhere.\n\nSorry, I’m probably not answering right. I guess I haven’t paid enough attention to know.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I mean, I don’t know. I say sorry a lot, if that counts, but sometimes that’s just because it feels like the easiest thing to say.\n\nLike at work, if something gets held up, I’ll kind of assume maybe I missed something, but then there are so many little steps that it’s hard to know whose part was whose. And then I start thinking about whether people are annoyed with me, but I don’t know if that’s guilt exactly or just… overthinking.\n\nSo, yeah, maybe sometimes, but I’m not sure I’d say “often” in a clear way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. “Worthless” feels like a really big word, I guess.\n\nSometimes I’ll feel like I’m not really doing anything right, but then I’ll think about it too much and start comparing it to, like, work stuff or school stuff I didn’t finish, and then I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean. My cousin says people throw words like that around too easily, but she also says that about everything, like she got really mad once about how people say “literally” wrong, which… sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nI don’t know if I’d say I often feel worthless. Maybe I get down on myself, but I’m not sure how to answer that clearly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },

Like, I remember I used to be more… I don’t know, lighter maybe. But I can’t really pin down if it’s the things themselves or just me being tired of everything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you currently find pleasure in any activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… sometimes I’ll do things because they’re there, like put on a show or go along if someone invites me somewhere, but I don’t know if I’d call it pleasure exactly.\n\nIt’s weird, because people will say, “Oh, wasn’t that fun?” and I’ll kind of agree, but then I’m thinking about how I need to do laundry or whether I forgot something at work. At my job, we have to check tiny details over and over, and I guess my brain gets stuck like that even outside of work. So… I don’t know. Maybe a little sometimes, but I can’t really tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and still find pleasure in now?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can name one clearly. Like, I used to do little things, you know, watch shows, go out with people sometimes, church stuff, maybe just sitting around talking—but now when I do them, I’m kind of half there.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about random things, like whether I sorted something wrong at work or if I left my clothes in the washer too long, and it’s like the moment passes before I can tell if I was enjoying it. So… I don’t know. I don’t really have a good answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t like saying it that way, because it sounds like I just stopped caring, and I didn’t mean to.\n\nBut I’ve mostly stopped going to basketball and the dance nights, and I used to actually look forward to those. And the church STEM mentoring thing too—I cared about that a lot, like helping the kids with little projects and stuff. I even bought these markers once because the regular ones were all dried out, and then I never brought them because I didn’t go that week… or the next.\n\nAnd work has been affected too. I’ve gotten written warnings because I’ve missed shifts and turned in inspection paperwork late. It’s like things I used to keep up with or care about are just… slipping, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly.\n\nI mean, sometimes when everything feels piled up, I’ll think things like I wish I could just not have to deal with any of it for a while, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you mean. Like, not wake up, or harming myself… I’m not sure. I don’t really sit there and make a plan or anything—sorry, I’m kind of getting stuck on the wording.\n\nIt’s hard because I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I left something unfinished at work, and then I don’t even know what I was feeling in the first place. So… I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re asking directly. I don’t… I don’t think I can give a clean yes or no.\n\nSometimes I get really overwhelmed and I have thoughts that scare me a little, but then I start wondering if I mean “harm myself” or if I just mean I want everything to stop for a bit. And then my brain goes off to something stupid, like whether I forgot to submit a form at work, and I lose track of what I was even trying to say.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really sorted it out in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “end my life” sounds so definite, and I don’t know if what I have counts as that.\n\nSometimes I get this feeling like I just want everything to stop, like I want to disappear from all the noise and expectations for a while. But then I start thinking about, like, whether I’m even answering you right, or if I’m making it sound worse than it is, and then my brain jumps to some random thing I need to do later.\n\nSo… I’m not trying to avoid the question. I just don’t feel sure how to answer it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself consistently, but my work pants have been fitting different from month to month, which is kind of how I notice.\n\nIt’s mostly eating being all over the place. Like most days on shift I’ll just not eat until it’s almost over because nothing sounds good, and then other times I end up getting vending machine stuff late because I’m just drained and it’s there. The break room vending machine has these chips I keep buying even though I don’t even really want them, and then I feel weird about it after.\n\nSo, yeah, my weight’s been kind of up and down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed it in a clear way.\n\nLike, at work I’m moving around because I have to, checking things and standing there and doing the same steps over and over, so it’s hard to tell if I’m slower or just… tired of doing it. And sitting still, I mean, I’ll tap my foot sometimes, but I’ve kind of always done that, especially if I’m waiting somewhere.\n\nSorry, I’m probably not answering right. I guess I haven’t paid enough attention to know.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I mean, I don’t know. I say sorry a lot, if that counts, but sometimes that’s just because it feels like the easiest thing to say.\n\nLike at work, if something gets held up, I’ll kind of assume maybe I missed something, but then there are so many little steps that it’s hard to know whose part was whose. And then I start thinking about whether people are annoyed with me, but I don’t know if that’s guilt exactly or just… overthinking.\n\nSo, yeah, maybe sometimes, but I’m not sure I’d say “often” in a clear way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. “Worthless” feels like a really big word, I guess.\n\nSometimes I’ll feel like I’m not really doing anything right, but then I’ll think about it too much and start comparing it to, like, work stuff or school stuff I didn’t finish, and then I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean. My cousin says people throw words like that around too easily, but she also says that about everything, like she got really mad once about how people say “literally” wrong, which… sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nI don’t know if I’d say I often feel worthless. Maybe I get down on myself, but I’m not sure how to answer that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been restless or having trouble staying still?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I think I kind of answered this before, but maybe I’m mixing up the questions.\n\nLike, I do fidget sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s more than normal. If I’m standing around at work, I’ll shift my weight a lot, but everybody does that because the floor gets uncomfortable after a while. And when I’m sitting, I’ll mess with my sleeves or my phone case or whatever, but I’ve done that since classes too, especially when lectures got long. There was this one professor who would always click his pen while talking, and then I’d notice everyone else’s little noises, and then I’d get distracted by that.\n\nSorry. I guess I haven’t noticed feeling especially restless, not in a way I could say for sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

The break room vending machine has these chips I keep buying even though I don’t even really want them, and then I feel weird about it after.\n\nSo, yeah, my weight’s been kind of up and down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed it in a clear way.\n\nLike, at work I’m moving around because I have to, checking things and standing there and doing the same steps over and over, so it’s hard to tell if I’m slower or just… tired of doing it. Even when I’m at work, or at church, or with people I care about, it doesn’t really lift.  \n\nI kept thinking maybe I was just tired or having a bad week, but… it’s been way more than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before this heavy, empty feeling started?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know, honestly. I haven’t really thought about it that way. I mean, I still do some things because they’re part of my routine, like going where I’m supposed to go, seeing people when I need to… but whether I enjoy them like before, I’m not sure.\n\nIt’s kind of hard to compare, because “before” feels blurry. Like, I remember I used to be more… I don’t know, lighter maybe. But I can’t really pin down if it’s the things themselves or just me being tired of everything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you currently find pleasure in any activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… sometimes I’ll do things because they’re there, like put on a show or go along if someone invites me somewhere, but I don’t know if I’d call it pleasure exactly.\n\nIt’s weird, because people will say, “Oh, wasn’t that fun?” and I’ll kind of agree, but then I’m thinking about how I need to do laundry or whether I forgot something at work. At my job, we have to check tiny details over and over, and I guess my brain gets stuck like that even outside of work. So… I don’t know. Maybe a little sometimes, but I can’t really tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and still find pleasure in now?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can name one clearly. Like, I used to do little things, you know, watch shows, go out with people sometimes, church stuff, maybe just sitting around talking—but now when I do them, I’m kind of half there.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about random things, like whether I sorted something wrong at work or if I left my clothes in the washer too long, and it’s like the moment passes before I can tell if I was enjoying it. So… I don’t know. I don’t really have a good answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t like saying it that way, because it sounds like I just stopped caring, and I didn’t mean to.\n\nBut I’ve mostly stopped going to basketball and the dance nights, and I used to actually look forward to those. And the church STEM mentoring thing too—I cared about that a lot, like helping the kids with little projects and stuff. I even bought these markers once because the regular ones were all dried out, and then I never brought them because I didn’t go that week… or the next.\n\nAnd work has been affected too. I’ve gotten written warnings because I’ve missed shifts and turned in inspection paperwork late. It’s like things I used to keep up with or care about are just… slipping, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly.\n\nI mean, sometimes when everything feels piled up, I’ll think things like I wish I could just not have to deal with any of it for a while, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you mean. Like, not wake up, or harming myself… I’m not sure. I don’t really sit there and make a plan or anything—sorry, I’m kind of getting stuck on the wording.\n\nIt’s hard because I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I left something unfinished at work, and then I don’t even know what I was feeling in the first place. So… I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re asking directly. I don’t… I don’t think I can give a clean yes or no.\n\nSometimes I get really overwhelmed and I have thoughts that scare me a little, but then I start wondering if I mean “harm myself” or if I just mean I want everything to stop for a bit. And then my brain goes off to something stupid, like whether I forgot to submit a form at work, and I lose track of what I was even trying to say.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really sorted it out in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “end my life” sounds so definite, and I don’t know if what I have counts as that.\n\nSometimes I get this feeling like I just want everything to stop, like I want to disappear from all the noise and expectations for a while. But then I start thinking about, like, whether I’m even answering you right, or if I’m making it sound worse than it is, and then my brain jumps to some random thing I need to do later.\n\nSo… I’m not trying to avoid the question. I just don’t feel sure how to answer it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself consistently, but my work pants have been fitting different from month to month, which is kind of how I notice.\n\nIt’s mostly eating being all over the place. Like most days on shift I’ll just not eat until it’s almost over because nothing sounds good, and then other times I end up getting vending machine stuff late because I’m just drained and it’s there. The break room vending machine has these chips I keep buying even though I don’t even really want them, and then I feel weird about it after.\n\nSo, yeah, my weight’s been kind of up and down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed it in a clear way.\n\nLike, at work I’m moving around because I have to, checking things and standing there and doing the same steps over and over, so it’s hard to tell if I’m slower or just… tired of doing it. And sitting still, I mean, I’ll tap my foot sometimes, but I’ve kind of always done that, especially if I’m waiting somewhere.\n\nSorry, I’m probably not answering right. I guess I haven’t paid enough attention to know.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I mean, I don’t know. I say sorry a lot, if that counts, but sometimes that’s just because it feels like the easiest thing to say.\n\nLike at work, if something gets held up, I’ll kind of assume maybe I missed something, but then there are so many little steps that it’s hard to know whose part was whose. And then I start thinking about whether people are annoyed with me, but I don’t know if that’s guilt exactly or just… overthinking.\n\nSo, yeah, maybe sometimes, but I’m not sure I’d say “often” in a clear way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. “Worthless” feels like a really big word, I guess.\n\nSometimes I’ll feel like I’m not really doing anything right, but then I’ll think about it too much and start comparing it to, like, work stuff or school stuff I didn’t finish, and then I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean. My cousin says people throw words like that around too easily, but she also says that about everything, like she got really mad once about how people say “literally” wrong, which… sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nI don’t know if I’d say I often feel worthless. Maybe I get down on myself, but I’m not sure how to answer that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been restless or having trouble staying still?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I think I kind of answered this before, but maybe I’m mixing up the questions.\n\nLike, I do fidget sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s more than normal. If I’m standing around at work, I’ll shift my weight a lot, but everybody does that because the floor gets uncomfortable after a while. And when I’m sitting, I’ll mess with my sleeves or my phone case or whatever, but I’ve done that since classes too, especially when lectures got long. There was this one professor who would always click his pen while talking, and then I’d notice everyone else’s little noises, and then I’d get distracted by that.\n\nSorry. I guess I haven’t noticed feeling especially restless, not in a way I could say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: Slower… I don’t know, maybe? I haven’t really clocked it, I guess.\n\nAt work I sometimes feel like I’m taking longer with little things, but then it depends on the batch or what needs checking, and some days the labels are weird or the scale is being annoying, so it’s hard to tell if it’s me or just the job. And I walk pretty normal, I think. My mom walks really fast everywhere, so compared to her I’ve always looked slow, even when I was in high school.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’d say I’ve noticed myself moving slower than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that weren't there when you were alone?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not like, hearing a voice talking to me or seeing a person there when nobody is there.\n\nSometimes when I’m alone, I’ll hear a noise and think it’s someone calling from another room, but then it’s usually the TV next door or something outside. My apartment has weird pipes too, and once I thought someone knocked but it was probably the upstairs neighbor dropping something. They’re always moving furniture at the strangest times, which I don’t understand, like how much furniture can one person have—\n\nSorry. I guess I’m not sure what counts, but I haven’t really had voices or seen things that weren’t there in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to die?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, “wanting to die” sounds really direct, and I don’t know if I’ve had it like that.\n\nSometimes people say things like “I’m so done” when they’re stressed, like at work when the machine jams and then someone uses the last good marker and doesn’t put it back—which is stupid, but it happens a lot. I don’t know, I guess I’d have to think about what you mean exactly.\n\nI’m not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

My mom walks really fast everywhere, so compared to her I’ve always looked slow, even when I was in high school.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’d say I’ve noticed myself moving slower than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that weren't there when you were alone?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not like, hearing a voice talking to me or seeing a person there when nobody is there.\n\nSometimes when I’m alone, I’ll hear a noise and think it’s someone calling from another room, but then it’s usually the TV next door or something outside. My apartment has weird pipes too, and once I thought someone knocked but it was probably the upstairs neighbor dropping something. They’re always moving furniture at the strangest times, which I don’t understand, like how much furniture can one person have—\n\nSorry. I guess I’m not sure what counts, but I haven’t really had voices or seen things that weren’t there in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to die?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, “wanting to die” sounds really direct, and I don’t know if I’ve had it like that.\n\nSometimes people say things like “I’m so done” when they’re stressed, like at work when the machine jams and then someone uses the last good marker and doesn’t put it back—which is stupid, but it happens a lot. I don’t know, I guess I’d have to think about what you mean exactly.\n\nI’m not sure.\n\n[Last Question]\nHow long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 20 / inspector_tester_sorter_sampler_or_weigher / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Duration\nmanifestation: \"This has been going on for about two years and four months, since I had to stop taking community-college classes because of money and my work schedule.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s been… a long time. I think about two years and a few months—like, two years and four months maybe.

It kind of started after I had to stop taking community college classes. It wasn’t because I wanted to stop, it was money and my work schedule, and the classes I needed were always at weird times, or I’d get assigned shifts that made it impossible. I kept thinking I’d go back the next semester, but then the next semester came and it just… didn’t happen.

So yeah, around then. About two years and four months.

I haven’t really thought about it that way. Like, I remember I used to be more… I don’t know, lighter maybe. But I can’t really pin down if it’s the things themselves or just me being tired of everything. So… I don’t know. I don’t really have a good answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t like saying it that way, because it sounds like I just stopped caring, and I didn’t mean to.\n\nBut I’ve mostly stopped going to basketball and the dance nights, and I used to actually look forward to those. I’ve gotten written warnings because I’ve missed shifts and turned in inspection paperwork late. It’s like things I used to keep up with or care about are just… slipping, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly.\n\nI mean, sometimes when everything feels piled up, I’ll think things like I wish I could just not have to deal with any of it for a while, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you mean. I don’t really sit there and make a plan or anything—sorry, I’m kind of getting stuck on the wording.\n\nIt’s hard because I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I left something unfinished at work, and then I don’t even know what I was feeling in the first place. So… I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re asking directly. I don’t… I don’t think I can give a clean yes or no.\n\nSometimes I get really overwhelmed and I have thoughts that scare me a little, but then I start wondering if I mean “harm myself” or if I just mean I want everything to stop for a bit. And then my brain goes off to something stupid, like whether I forgot to submit a form at work, and I lose track of what I was even trying to say.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really sorted it out in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “end my life” sounds so definite, and I don’t know if what I have counts as that.\n\nSometimes I get this feeling like I just want everything to stop, like I want to disappear from all the noise and expectations for a while. But then I start thinking about, like, whether I’m even answering you right, or if I’m making it sound worse than it is, and then my brain jumps to some random thing I need to do later.\n\nSo… I’m not trying to avoid the question. I just don’t feel sure how to answer it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself consistently, but my work pants have been fitting different from month to month, which is kind of how I notice.\n\nIt’s mostly eating being all over the place. Like most days on shift I’ll just not eat until it’s almost over because nothing sounds good, and then other times I end up getting vending machine stuff late because I’m just drained and it’s there. The break room vending machine has these chips I keep buying even though I don’t even really want them, and then I feel weird about it after.\n\nSo, yeah, my weight’s been kind of up and down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed it in a clear way.\n\nLike, at work I’m moving around because I have to, checking things and standing there and doing the same steps over and over, so it’s hard to tell if I’m slower or just… tired of doing it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… I don’t think I’m fidgeting all the time. I mean, I do move around sometimes, like tapping my foot if I’m sitting somewhere too long, but I feel like a lot of people do that.

At work I’m usually using my hands anyway, so it’s hard to tell what counts as restless and what’s just… doing the job. And the chairs in the break room are really uncomfortable, so I shift around there, but that might just be the chairs. They’re those hard plastic ones, and one of them wobbles, and everyone still sits in it even though it’s obviously the bad chair.

Sorry. I guess I haven’t really noticed being restless most of the time.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3, F32.8, F33.0, F33.1, F33.2, F33.3, F33.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve just felt really low for a long time. Like, for the last couple years there’s this heavy, empty feeling that kind of follows me around. Even when I’m at work, or at church, or with people I care about, it doesn’t really lift.  \n\nI kept thinking maybe I was just tired or having a bad week, but… it’s been way more than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before this heavy, empty feeling started?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know, honestly. I haven’t really thought about it that way. I mean, I still do some things because they’re part of my routine, like going where I’m supposed to go, seeing people when I need to… but whether I enjoy them like before, I’m not sure.\n\nIt’s kind of hard to compare, because “before” feels blurry. Like, I remember I used to be more… I don’t know, lighter maybe. But I can’t really pin down if it’s the things themselves or just me being tired of everything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you currently find pleasure in any activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… sometimes I’ll do things because they’re there, like put on a show or go along if someone invites me somewhere, but I don’t know if I’d call it pleasure exactly.\n\nIt’s weird, because people will say, “Oh, wasn’t that fun?” and I’ll kind of agree, but then I’m thinking about how I need to do laundry or whether I forgot something at work. At my job, we have to check tiny details over and over, and I guess my brain gets stuck like that even outside of work. So… I don’t know. Maybe a little sometimes, but I can’t really tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and still find pleasure in now?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can name one clearly. Like, I used to do little things, you know, watch shows, go out with people sometimes, church stuff, maybe just sitting around talking—but now when I do them, I’m kind of half there.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about random things, like whether I sorted something wrong at work or if I left my clothes in the washer too long, and it’s like the moment passes before I can tell if I was enjoying it. So… I don’t know. I don’t really have a good answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t like saying it that way, because it sounds like I just stopped caring, and I didn’t mean to.\n\nBut I’ve mostly stopped going to basketball and the dance nights, and I used to actually look forward to those. And the church STEM mentoring thing too—I cared about that a lot, like helping the kids with little projects and stuff. I even bought these markers once because the regular ones were all dried out, and then I never brought them because I didn’t go that week… or the next.\n\nAnd work has been affected too. I’ve gotten written warnings because I’ve missed shifts and turned in inspection paperwork late. It’s like things I used to keep up with or care about are just… slipping, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly.\n\nI mean, sometimes when everything feels piled up, I’ll think things like I wish I could just not have to deal with any of it for a while, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you mean. Like, not wake up, or harming myself… I’m not sure. I don’t really sit there and make a plan or anything—sorry, I’m kind of getting stuck on the wording.\n\nIt’s hard because I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I left something unfinished at work, and then I don’t even know what I was feeling in the first place. So… I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re asking directly. I don’t… I don’t think I can give a clean yes or no.\n\nSometimes I get really overwhelmed and I have thoughts that scare me a little, but then I start wondering if I mean “harm myself” or if I just mean I want everything to stop for a bit. And then my brain goes off to something stupid, like whether I forgot to submit a form at work, and I lose track of what I was even trying to say.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really sorted it out in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “end my life” sounds so definite, and I don’t know if what I have counts as that.\n\nSometimes I get this feeling like I just want everything to stop, like I want to disappear from all the noise and expectations for a while. But then I start thinking about, like, whether I’m even answering you right, or if I’m making it sound worse than it is, and then my brain jumps to some random thing I need to do later.\n\nSo… I’m not trying to avoid the question. I just don’t feel sure how to answer it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself consistently, but my work pants have been fitting different from month to month, which is kind of how I notice.\n\nIt’s mostly eating being all over the place. Like most days on shift I’ll just not eat until it’s almost over because nothing sounds good, and then other times I end up getting vending machine stuff late because I’m just drained and it’s there. The break room vending machine has these chips I keep buying even though I don’t even really want them, and then I feel weird about it after.\n\nSo, yeah, my weight’s been kind of up and down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed it in a clear way.\n\nLike, at work I’m moving around because I have to, checking things and standing there and doing the same steps over and over, so it’s hard to tell if I’m slower or just… tired of doing it. And sitting still, I mean, I’ll tap my foot sometimes, but I’ve kind of always done that, especially if I’m waiting somewhere.\n\nSorry, I’m probably not answering right. I guess I haven’t paid enough attention to know.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I mean, I don’t know. I say sorry a lot, if that counts, but sometimes that’s just because it feels like the easiest thing to say.\n\nLike at work, if something gets held up, I’ll kind of assume maybe I missed something, but then there are so many little steps that it’s hard to know whose part was whose. And then I start thinking about whether people are annoyed with me, but I don’t know if that’s guilt exactly or just… overthinking.\n\nSo, yeah, maybe sometimes, but I’m not sure I’d say “often” in a clear way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. “Worthless” feels like a really big word, I guess.\n\nSometimes I’ll feel like I’m not really doing anything right, but then I’ll think about it too much and start comparing it to, like, work stuff or school stuff I didn’t finish, and then I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean. My cousin says people throw words like that around too easily, but she also says that about everything, like she got really mad once about how people say “literally” wrong, which… sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nI don’t know if I’d say I often feel worthless. Maybe I get down on myself, but I’m not sure how to answer that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been restless or having trouble staying still?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I think I kind of answered this before, but maybe I’m mixing up the questions.\n\nLike, I do fidget sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s more than normal. If I’m standing around at work, I’ll shift my weight a lot, but everybody does that because the floor gets uncomfortable after a while. And when I’m sitting, I’ll mess with my sleeves or my phone case or whatever, but I’ve done that since classes too, especially when lectures got long. There was this one professor who would always click his pen while talking, and then I’d notice everyone else’s little noises, and then I’d get distracted by that.\n\nSorry. I guess I haven’t noticed feeling especially restless, not in a way I could say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: Slower… I don’t know, maybe? I haven’t really clocked it, I guess.\n\nAt work I sometimes feel like I’m taking longer with little things, but then it depends on the batch or what needs checking, and some days the labels are weird or the scale is being annoying, so it’s hard to tell if it’s me or just the job. And I walk pretty normal, I think. My mom walks really fast everywhere, so compared to her I’ve always looked slow, even when I was in high school.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’d say I’ve noticed myself moving slower than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that weren't there when you were alone?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not like, hearing a voice talking to me or seeing a person there when nobody is there.\n\nSometimes when I’m alone, I’ll hear a noise and think it’s someone calling from another room, but then it’s usually the TV next door or something outside. My apartment has weird pipes too, and once I thought someone knocked but it was probably the upstairs neighbor dropping something. They’re always moving furniture at the strangest times, which I don’t understand, like how much furniture can one person have—\n\nSorry. About two years and four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or restless most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think I’m fidgeting all the time. I haven’t really sorted it out in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “end my life” sounds so definite, and I don’t know if what I have counts as that.\n\nSometimes I get this feeling like I just want everything to stop, like I want to disappear from all the noise and expectations for a while. But then I start thinking about, like, whether I’m even answering you right, or if I’m making it sound worse than it is, and then my brain jumps to some random thing I need to do later.\n\nSo… I’m not trying to avoid the question. I just don’t feel sure how to answer it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself consistently, but my work pants have been fitting different from month to month, which is kind of how I notice.\n\nIt’s mostly eating being all over the place. Like most days on shift I’ll just not eat until it’s almost over because nothing sounds good, and then other times I end up getting vending machine stuff late because I’m just drained and it’s there. The break room vending machine has these chips I keep buying even though I don’t even really want them, and then I feel weird about it after.\n\nSo, yeah, my weight’s been kind of up and down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed it in a clear way.\n\nLike, at work I’m moving around because I have to, checking things and standing there and doing the same steps over and over, so it’s hard to tell if I’m slower or just… tired of doing it. And sitting still, I mean, I’ll tap my foot sometimes, but I’ve kind of always done that, especially if I’m waiting somewhere.\n\nSorry, I’m probably not answering right. I guess I haven’t paid enough attention to know.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I mean, I don’t know. I say sorry a lot, if that counts, but sometimes that’s just because it feels like the easiest thing to say.\n\nLike at work, if something gets held up, I’ll kind of assume maybe I missed something, but then there are so many little steps that it’s hard to know whose part was whose. And then I start thinking about whether people are annoyed with me, but I don’t know if that’s guilt exactly or just… overthinking.\n\nSo, yeah, maybe sometimes, but I’m not sure I’d say “often” in a clear way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. “Worthless” feels like a really big word, I guess.\n\nSometimes I’ll feel like I’m not really doing anything right, but then I’ll think about it too much and start comparing it to, like, work stuff or school stuff I didn’t finish, and then I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean. My cousin says people throw words like that around too easily, but she also says that about everything, like she got really mad once about how people say “literally” wrong, which… sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nI don’t know if I’d say I often feel worthless. Maybe I get down on myself, but I’m not sure how to answer that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been restless or having trouble staying still?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I think I kind of answered this before, but maybe I’m mixing up the questions.\n\nLike, I do fidget sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s more than normal. If I’m standing around at work, I’ll shift my weight a lot, but everybody does that because the floor gets uncomfortable after a while. And when I’m sitting, I’ll mess with my sleeves or my phone case or whatever, but I’ve done that since classes too, especially when lectures got long. There was this one professor who would always click his pen while talking, and then I’d notice everyone else’s little noises, and then I’d get distracted by that.\n\nSorry. I guess I haven’t noticed feeling especially restless, not in a way I could say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: Slower… I don’t know, maybe? I haven’t really clocked it, I guess.\n\nAt work I sometimes feel like I’m taking longer with little things, but then it depends on the batch or what needs checking, and some days the labels are weird or the scale is being annoying, so it’s hard to tell if it’s me or just the job. And I walk pretty normal, I think. My mom walks really fast everywhere, so compared to her I’ve always looked slow, even when I was in high school.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’d say I’ve noticed myself moving slower than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that weren't there when you were alone?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not like, hearing a voice talking to me or seeing a person there when nobody is there.\n\nSometimes when I’m alone, I’ll hear a noise and think it’s someone calling from another room, but then it’s usually the TV next door or something outside. My apartment has weird pipes too, and once I thought someone knocked but it was probably the upstairs neighbor dropping something. They’re always moving furniture at the strangest times, which I don’t understand, like how much furniture can one person have—\n\nSorry. I guess I’m not sure what counts, but I haven’t really had voices or seen things that weren’t there in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to die?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, “wanting to die” sounds really direct, and I don’t know if I’ve had it like that.\n\nSometimes people say things like “I’m so done” when they’re stressed, like at work when the machine jams and then someone uses the last good marker and doesn’t put it back—which is stupid, but it happens a lot. I don’t know, I guess I’d have to think about what you mean exactly.\n\nI’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a long time. I think about two years and a few months—like, two years and four months maybe.\n\nIt kind of started after I had to stop taking community college classes. It wasn’t because I wanted to stop, it was money and my work schedule, and the classes I needed were always at weird times, or I’d get assigned shifts that made it impossible. I kept thinking I’d go back the next semester, but then the next semester came and it just… didn’t happen.\n\nSo yeah, around then. About two years and four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or restless most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think I’m fidgeting all the time. I mean, I do move around sometimes, like tapping my foot if I’m sitting somewhere too long, but I feel like a lot of people do that.\n\nAt work I’m usually using my hands anyway, so it’s hard to tell what counts as restless and what’s just… doing the job. And the chairs in the break room are really uncomfortable, so I shift around there, but that might just be the chairs. They’re those hard plastic ones, and one of them wobbles, and everyone still sits in it even though it’s obviously the bad chair.\n\nSorry. I guess I haven’t really noticed being restless most of the time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve just felt really low for a long time. Like, for the last couple years there’s this heavy, empty feeling that kind of follows me around. Even when I’m at work, or at church, or with people I care about, it doesn’t really lift.  \n\nI kept thinking maybe I was just tired or having a bad week, but… it’s been way more than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before this heavy, empty feeling started?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know, honestly. I haven’t really thought about it that way. I mean, I still do some things because they’re part of my routine, like going where I’m supposed to go, seeing people when I need to… but whether I enjoy them like before, I’m not sure.\n\nIt’s kind of hard to compare, because “before” feels blurry. Like, I remember I used to be more… I don’t know, lighter maybe. But I can’t really pin down if it’s the things themselves or just me being tired of everything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you currently find pleasure in any activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… sometimes I’ll do things because they’re there, like put on a show or go along if someone invites me somewhere, but I don’t know if I’d call it pleasure exactly.\n\nIt’s weird, because people will say, “Oh, wasn’t that fun?” and I’ll kind of agree, but then I’m thinking about how I need to do laundry or whether I forgot something at work. At my job, we have to check tiny details over and over, and I guess my brain gets stuck like that even outside of work. So… I don’t know. Maybe a little sometimes, but I can’t really tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and still find pleasure in now?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can name one clearly. Like, I used to do little things, you know, watch shows, go out with people sometimes, church stuff, maybe just sitting around talking—but now when I do them, I’m kind of half there.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about random things, like whether I sorted something wrong at work or if I left my clothes in the washer too long, and it’s like the moment passes before I can tell if I was enjoying it. So… I don’t know. I don’t really have a good answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t like saying it that way, because it sounds like I just stopped caring, and I didn’t mean to.\n\nBut I’ve mostly stopped going to basketball and the dance nights, and I used to actually look forward to those. And the church STEM mentoring thing too—I cared about that a lot, like helping the kids with little projects and stuff. I even bought these markers once because the regular ones were all dried out, and then I never brought them because I didn’t go that week… or the next.\n\nAnd work has been affected too. I’ve gotten written warnings because I’ve missed shifts and turned in inspection paperwork late. It’s like things I used to keep up with or care about are just… slipping, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly.\n\nI mean, sometimes when everything feels piled up, I’ll think things like I wish I could just not have to deal with any of it for a while, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as what you mean. Like, not wake up, or harming myself… I’m not sure. I don’t really sit there and make a plan or anything—sorry, I’m kind of getting stuck on the wording.\n\nIt’s hard because I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I left something unfinished at work, and then I don’t even know what I was feeling in the first place. So… I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re asking directly. I don’t… I don’t think I can give a clean yes or no.\n\nSometimes I get really overwhelmed and I have thoughts that scare me a little, but then I start wondering if I mean “harm myself” or if I just mean I want everything to stop for a bit. And then my brain goes off to something stupid, like whether I forgot to submit a form at work, and I lose track of what I was even trying to say.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really sorted it out in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “end my life” sounds so definite, and I don’t know if what I have counts as that.\n\nSometimes I get this feeling like I just want everything to stop, like I want to disappear from all the noise and expectations for a while. But then I start thinking about, like, whether I’m even answering you right, or if I’m making it sound worse than it is, and then my brain jumps to some random thing I need to do later.\n\nSo… I’m not trying to avoid the question. I just don’t feel sure how to answer it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really weigh myself consistently, but my work pants have been fitting different from month to month, which is kind of how I notice.\n\nIt’s mostly eating being all over the place. Like most days on shift I’ll just not eat until it’s almost over because nothing sounds good, and then other times I end up getting vending machine stuff late because I’m just drained and it’s there. The break room vending machine has these chips I keep buying even though I don’t even really want them, and then I feel weird about it after.\n\nSo, yeah, my weight’s been kind of up and down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve noticed it in a clear way.\n\nLike, at work I’m moving around because I have to, checking things and standing there and doing the same steps over and over, so it’s hard to tell if I’m slower or just… tired of doing it. And sitting still, I mean, I’ll tap my foot sometimes, but I’ve kind of always done that, especially if I’m waiting somewhere.\n\nSorry, I’m probably not answering right. I guess I haven’t paid enough attention to know.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I mean, I don’t know. I say sorry a lot, if that counts, but sometimes that’s just because it feels like the easiest thing to say.\n\nLike at work, if something gets held up, I’ll kind of assume maybe I missed something, but then there are so many little steps that it’s hard to know whose part was whose. And then I start thinking about whether people are annoyed with me, but I don’t know if that’s guilt exactly or just… overthinking.\n\nSo, yeah, maybe sometimes, but I’m not sure I’d say “often” in a clear way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. “Worthless” feels like a really big word, I guess.\n\nSometimes I’ll feel like I’m not really doing anything right, but then I’ll think about it too much and start comparing it to, like, work stuff or school stuff I didn’t finish, and then I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean. My cousin says people throw words like that around too easily, but she also says that about everything, like she got really mad once about how people say “literally” wrong, which… sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nI don’t know if I’d say I often feel worthless. Maybe I get down on myself, but I’m not sure how to answer that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been restless or having trouble staying still?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I think I kind of answered this before, but maybe I’m mixing up the questions.\n\nLike, I do fidget sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s more than normal. If I’m standing around at work, I’ll shift my weight a lot, but everybody does that because the floor gets uncomfortable after a while. And when I’m sitting, I’ll mess with my sleeves or my phone case or whatever, but I’ve done that since classes too, especially when lectures got long. There was this one professor who would always click his pen while talking, and then I’d notice everyone else’s little noises, and then I’d get distracted by that.\n\nSorry. I guess I haven’t noticed feeling especially restless, not in a way I could say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: Slower… I don’t know, maybe? I haven’t really clocked it, I guess.\n\nAt work I sometimes feel like I’m taking longer with little things, but then it depends on the batch or what needs checking, and some days the labels are weird or the scale is being annoying, so it’s hard to tell if it’s me or just the job. And I walk pretty normal, I think. My mom walks really fast everywhere, so compared to her I’ve always looked slow, even when I was in high school.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’d say I’ve noticed myself moving slower than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that weren't there when you were alone?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not like, hearing a voice talking to me or seeing a person there when nobody is there.\n\nSometimes when I’m alone, I’ll hear a noise and think it’s someone calling from another room, but then it’s usually the TV next door or something outside. My apartment has weird pipes too, and once I thought someone knocked but it was probably the upstairs neighbor dropping something. They’re always moving furniture at the strangest times, which I don’t understand, like how much furniture can one person have—\n\nSorry. I guess I’m not sure what counts, but I haven’t really had voices or seen things that weren’t there in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to die?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, “wanting to die” sounds really direct, and I don’t know if I’ve had it like that.\n\nSometimes people say things like “I’m so done” when they’re stressed, like at work when the machine jams and then someone uses the last good marker and doesn’t put it back—which is stupid, but it happens a lot. I don’t know, I guess I’d have to think about what you mean exactly.\n\nI’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a long time. I think about two years and a few months—like, two years and four months maybe.\n\nIt kind of started after I had to stop taking community college classes. It wasn’t because I wanted to stop, it was money and my work schedule, and the classes I needed were always at weird times, or I’d get assigned shifts that made it impossible. I kept thinking I’d go back the next semester, but then the next semester came and it just… didn’t happen.\n\nSo yeah, around then. About two years and four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or restless most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think I’m fidgeting all the time. I mean, I do move around sometimes, like tapping my foot if I’m sitting somewhere too long, but I feel like a lot of people do that.\n\nAt work I’m usually using my hands anyway, so it’s hard to tell what counts as restless and what’s just… doing the job. And the chairs in the break room are really uncomfortable, so I shift around there, but that might just be the chairs. They’re those hard plastic ones, and one of them wobbles, and everyone still sits in it even though it’s obviously the bad chair.\n\nSorry. I guess I haven’t really noticed being restless most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about ending your life in the past few months?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I’m not sure. Like, ending my life sounds really specific, and I don’t know if what I’ve thought counts as that or not.\n\nSometimes my brain kind of goes blank when people ask it that way, because I start thinking about the exact wording, like if “ending” means making a plan, or just having a thought pop in and then disappear. Sorry, I know that’s probably not helpful. It’s just hard to answer yes or no.\n\nI guess I don’t really have a clear answer right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about harming yourself in the past week?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. Like, “harming myself” can mean a lot of things, right? Sometimes I think of it as like actually doing something, and sometimes people mean even just… thoughts that show up.\n\nThis past week has kind of blurred together, honestly. I remember work being really annoying because one of the bins kept getting mislabeled and everyone was blaming everyone else, and I kept thinking I was going to get pulled into it even though it wasn’t mine.\n\nSorry, that’s not really what you asked. I guess I’m not sure. I don’t have a clear answer right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",

    "duration_verified": "symptoms present for over 2 years",
    "functional_impairment": true,
    "traumatic_stressor": null,
    "psychosocial_stressor": null,
    "additional_requirements": ["Onset after stopping community college classes due to financial and scheduling issues"]
  }
}
